
Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

  1071	## 16. PAGE3: finish the ordinary private-page plans
  1072	
  1073	Commissioned 2026-10-13; implemented locally. Muse and fresh Codex both
  1074	READY in round 5 at `65d505289f`; verbatim reviews and frozen brief are
  1075	`llp/reviews/3000.036-page3.r5.{muse,codex,brief}.md`.
  1076	PAGE2 left 18 notes in 11 of the 50 odd wave-10 final schemas: 12
  1077	party/static-grant pages, three compound rules, two hidden parent sources
  1078	and one pure membership. This amendment extends the existing plan rather
  1079	than asking builders to merge lists themselves. It changes no read authority,
  1080	replicated data or runtime resource ceiling.
  1081	
  1082	**Static grants and whole-group reads.** An owner-or-readable-static-grant
  1083	rule uses the existing party/grant merge on synced union tables too. The
  1084	server evaluates the positive readable grant once; its holder reads the
  1085	original whole range, everyone else the party ranges. An explicit `first` or
  1086	`last` is a page bound, not a table cap: a role holder may page an unbounded
  1087	table, under the usual request meter. An implicit whole-group read is expanded
  1088	only if its original fixed prefix has an applicable stored cap or fully
  1089	pinned total unique, at or below the examined ceiling. That original bound
  1090	applies to the union. A cap on owner does not bound a staff's whole table.
  1091	A fully pinned total unique may supply a one-row lane with the implicit id
  1092	position, even when its physical index has no unpinned component.
  1093	
  1094	**Full union devices.** The same descriptor and scope serve server and device
  1095	cursors. On a device a full union target's grant lane reads its held authorized
  1096	rows, with the ordinary acquisition, coverage and meter checks; it does not
  1097	probe a remote grant. That held range covers every other target lane, so the
  1098	others are not opened. Missing acquisition stays unknown. Online-only and
  1099	single-arm narrowed sync retain their existing grant deferral. This amends
  1100	LLP 3000.036.000.000 §5.3's planner restriction, while preserving its holding
  1101	and acquisition invariants.
  1102	
  1103	**Pure membership.** Remove the syntactic party requirement from §15. A rule
  1104	`exists members[.id, viewer]` or `exists members[.parent, viewer]` has a system
  1105	range and exact membership descriptors, no party descriptors. All source
  1106	readability, unique/cap proofs, total filters, index/key bounds, shared
  1107	positions and discovery/head fences remain. If the source discovery is cut,
  1108	no target rows or cursor are emitted. An empty complete discovery means an
  1109	empty complete page. Point uniqueness still does not cap memberships.
  1110	
  1111	**Positive compound rules.** Distribute positive `and`/`or` into at most seven
  1112	alternatives plus the original system range. Each alternative retains all
  1113	of its row equality conditions. It contains either one exact membership
  1114	witness, or row equality pins and positive row-independent guards. A membership
  1115	conjunct supplies precisely its own authority; all remaining conjuncts must
  1116	be true by the target lane's pins. A pinned membership target instead uses
  1117	a readable page guard, without discovering hidden source rows. No negated
  1118	probe, arithmetic, fallible predicate or second varying source component is
  1119	admitted. Candidates are speculative and fall back when the physical plan
  1120	or bound-IR validator cannot prove the whole admitted shape.
  1121	
  1122	A new guarded-range descriptor evaluates `page_guard_decision` on its own
  1123	pins. A true readable guard opens only that range, not the other alternatives;
  1124	a false guard opens none. It does not cover other lanes. The system range
  1125	covers every lane only for the system. Status/party lanes, discovered parent
  1126	lanes and guarded lanes share one ordered id-tied position and deduplicate
  1127	there, as in §§3–6. Thus if `R = ∨i (Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi)` and W is the total page
  1128	predicate, each lane returns exactly `W ∧ Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi`; their union is W ∧ R.
  1129	Guard facts and membership sources must be readable, as in §15, so neither
  1130	empty results nor completeness reveal hidden witness rows. Read-meter cuts
  1131	and frontier rules are unchanged; heap exhaustion remains a refusal.
  1132	
  1133	A nullable small enum's `field != null` has a finite positive expansion into
  1134	its declared non-null values. This retains null exclusion exactly; ordinary
  1135	nullable inequalities against other values keep their original scan. The
  1136	wave-10 archive with four species therefore gets four enum ranges plus its
  1137	owner, readable reviewer and system ranges. Compound volunteer-open and
  1138	parent-teacher/status rules preserve each status condition when opening lanes.
  1139	
  1140	**Readable static predicates.** A `where` disjunction may repeat a readable
  1141	static point, such as `.gleaner = viewer or exists coordinators[viewer]`.
  1142	Every point-key expression must be an infallible, read-free name or literal
  1143	(including canonical signed numeric literals); arithmetic, assertions, calls
  1144	and member reads keep the original scan and short circuit. The point must be
  1145	a total unique with fixed terms, readable by those pins or
  1146	its identical self-witness rule, without a lifetime or read-through boundary.
  1147	It remains in the original predicate for every caller, including the system.
  1148	This extension belongs to static party/where branch merges only. Membership
  1149	discovery and compound-rule expansion retain their read-free total filter
  1150	requirement, mirrored separately by model cases 3 and 4. The physical point
  1151	can cut even though its logical predicate is total. The
  1152	branch frontier therefore excludes an interrupted filter candidate; it may
  1153	continue only from an earlier fully evaluated candidate, so retry cannot
  1154	skip the interrupted row. A source its holders cannot read keeps its scan.
  1155	
  1156	A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
  1157	on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
  1158	not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.
  1159	
  1160	**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v2` fences guarded descriptors,
  1161	held-union merge behavior and fully pinned point-lane admission; it is derived
  1162	from bound plans, serialized as an optional operation fact, and older engines
  1163	refuse it through the ordinary handshake. Existing descriptor bytes remain
  1164	unchanged when none of these new lane types is present. Membership-only plans
  1165	retain the existing membership capability rules. The Lean surface model
  1166	removes its party requirement, and models the admitted positive rule ranges.
  1167	Enums refuse the entire model module with the existing explicit `type-enum`
  1168	reason before any program is declared supported. Compound where-branch
  1169	composition, nullable constant pins and physically nonportable/ambiguous
  1170	compound plans have their own named emitter exclusions. Native static
  1171	predicate keys that are arguments or signed expression nodes use the named
  1172	`page-static-key-expression` model boundary until their separate readability
  1173	classifier is extended; they are unsupported, never counted as oracle passes;
  1174	unsupported fragments remain explicitly named rather than accepted by the
  1175	oracle. Its logical leaf soundness/coverage proof includes pure membership.
  1176	
  1177	**Hidden-source boundary and exact repairs.** PAGE3 does not scan private
  1178	parent rows whose owner cannot necessarily read them. No cap/index alone
  1179	repairs that absence proof. Copy the parent's immutable owner onto the child,
  1180	keep that copy and its parent reference immutable, keep the parent non-expiring,
  1181	and retain the reference with `on delete restrict`, and check the copy against the parent in the child's
  1182	insert rule (and in every trusted seed/backfill). Replace the parent-owner
  1183	read-rule leaf with the copied owner comparison. The copy preserves authority
  1184	only while the parent owner is immutable; a mutable owner needs transactional
  1185	propagation or the original rule. Existing data must be backfilled before
  1186	adoption. The diagnostic names the actual table, reference and owner column
  1187	and supplies exact source edits. It never recommends making parents public.
  1188	
  1189	**PAGE3 qualification and review dispositions (2026-10-14).** Round 1
  1190	repaired null-exempt bounds, parent reference/lifetime preservation, outer
  1191	conjunction guards and capability scope; round 2 repaired fallible static keys,
  1192	block-lowered probe fencing and defensive model bounds. Round 3 named static
  1193	argument/signed-key model exclusions, with zero-oracle Unsupported regressions.
  1194	Round 4 separated readable-static model filters from the read-free gates for
  1195	membership/compound plans, with actual native/model data and completeness
  1196	comparisons. Round 5's two independent reviewers marked the same revision
  1197	READY. No unsupported model case is counted as a pass.
  1198	
  1199	All 50 copied odd wave-10 final schemas compile: 18 notes in 11 schemas
  1200	become 2 in one (both hidden parents). Applying the exact checked copy repair
  1201	to a further copied corpus gives 0 in all 50. Per-shape counts, input/output
  1202	hashes and source edits are in `snapback4/reports/20261013-page3/`. Final
  1203	400-case campaigns have 22,277 native and 12,686 device executions without
  1204	disagreements; Lean's 1,108 executions retain only the three existing C2
  1205	pagination occurrences, with no new signature. Lean audits 59 theorems with
  1206	standard axioms only, runs 74 examples and seven fixtures. Focused language,

  1208

  1210	704,662 B gzip / 532,356 B brotli; budget 705,162 B = measured gzip + 500 B.
  1211	Family/absence audits and wasm/client builds pass. Later edits affect only

  1213	
  1214	## 17. PAGE4: complete multi-audience pages and exact scopes
  1215	
  1216	Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-11 fix #11. This amendment extends the
  1217	existing bounded planner. No read authority, replicated audience, runtime
  1218	ceiling or cursor codec changes. Query unions containing a public status
  1219	are separate from union *sync*: a public sync branch remains excluded.
  1220	
  1221	**Mixed positive alternatives.** Positive distribution also applies to a
  1222	simple disjunction mixing party equality, public/status equality, exact
  1223	membership and readable static grants. Each alternative retains every
  1224	conjunct and the complete original query predicate. Equality lanes,
  1225	guarded ranges and exact discovered membership lanes share the existing
  1226	ordered id-tied merge, with duplicate positions emitted once. At most seven
  1227	alternatives plus the system range fit. Speculative source/target indexes
  1228	are retained only when the bound validator accepts the whole plan.
  1229	
  1230	Named plain page indexes normalize to their declared column order only in
  1231	a speculative merge candidate. A declined candidate keeps its original read.
  1232	An exact owner/member rule whose varying component is necessarily pinned
  1233	can use the existing party/guard merge; alternative-only pins do not qualify.
  1234	
  1235	**Exact where witnesses.** A positive whole `where` conjunct identical to a
  1236	whole read-rule disjunct can define an exact narrower audience: for example
  1237	`posts where exists authors[.id, viewer] first 20 by .at after c`. Discover
  1238	that readable witness under §§14–16's total point and complete-discovery
  1239	proofs. Other audiences cannot contribute rows to W. Every other conjunct
  1240	must be read-free and total. The original predicate remains in admission
  1241	validation; the system descriptor retains the authored witness, because
  1242	system bypasses R but never W. Its prefix also binds the fixed target pins
  1243	into the shared cursor scope. A hidden source keeps the original scan.
  1244	
  1245	**Pinned components of exact witnesses.** A positive alternative may pin
  1246	one component of a multi-column rule probe. Substitute only a necessary
  1247	infallible fixed equality, leaving one varying source component; the core
  1248	validator independently checks the original field term against that exact
  1249	target prefix. Example: `.manager = viewer and exists reporting[report =
  1250	.report, manager = .manager]` discovers reporting rows with manager=viewer,
  1251	then their report values. The other party's alternative inverts the other
  1252	component. Extra named point equalities survive. No arithmetic, missing
  1253	pin, second varying component or hidden source is treated as proof.
  1254	
  1255	**Parent snapshots.** Selecting `s.id` or a stored scalar field of a required
  1256	point-read row image is a fixed lexical value, not a new row read. In a
  1257	standalone page's predicate (returned or assigned), the compiler binds those
  1258	selections once before planning. Optional rows, reference hops, private/maintained fields,
  1259	rebindings and joined paths keep the original predicate. A snapshot does
  1260	not prove that a child's copied band/manager equals its parent's field;
  1261	source repairs must say when their extra pins narrow the authored scope.
  1262	
  1263	**Membership word search.** The existing compact ordinary server search may
  1264	derive its exact two-component total membership audience on an online-only
  1265	table too. Provision the reverse readable source index and scoped words
  1266	index in one checked speculative candidate. Synced targets retain their
  1267	same exact audience requirement. The device program and sync holdings do
  1268	not acquire a predicate; empty needles retain the original browse arm.
  1269	Readability, non-expiring source, portable keys, opening meter refusal,
  1270	word-head merge and original cursor binding remain LLP 3000.028 §3.3's.
  1271	
  1272	**Honest incomplete forms.** A fallback note distinguishes an analyzer
  1273	limitation (the current admitted ranges cannot prove this positive shape)
  1274	from a truly partial read (its range/source cannot establish absence under
  1275	its declared authority). It names the exact complete line when one can be
  1276	verified; scope-narrowing pins say that they narrow the answer. A hidden
  1277	parent retains PAGE3's checked copy/backfill repair. No cap/index-only
  1278	suggestion is offered for an unreadable witness, and no copied relationship
  1279	is assumed from a successful parent read alone.
  1280	
  1281	**Readable negative static points and finite enums.** A negated static
  1282	probe is a page guard only when its selected key is total unique, its source
  1283	is readable at that key and has no maintained-state, read lifetime or read-through boundary.
  1284	Its absence is then an observable authorized fact. A hidden negative probe
  1285	still cannot open a complete range. Required enum inequality against a
  1286	literal expands to its exact remaining variants; optional enum inequality
  1287	against a non-null value stays residual. No varying negative source is
  1288	inverted. This completes the renovation selectionsFor page (its unpaged budget aggregation
  1289	retains an analyzer limitation) without
  1290	turning timed privacy into an unsupported absence claim.
  1291	
  1292	A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
  1293	on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
  1294	not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.
  1295	
  1296	**Compatibility and model.** Existing descriptor bytes and semantics serve
  1297	mixed unions and exact where witnesses. `branch-pages-v3` fences pinned multi-column witnesses, readable negative
  1298	static guarded/covering ranges and the compiler-owned `RequireUncapped`
  1299	primitive before an older validator or decoder can encounter them. A nullable
  1300	row assertion in a query checks the read meter after evaluating its point;
  1301	a cut raises the internal E_BOUND that the query boundary reports as an
  1302	unavailable incomplete answer, instead of inventing the author's absence error.
  1303	Ordinary absent/hidden rows still use the authored error, and mutation
  1304	assertions retain their transactional policy.
  1305	The independent Lean surface classifier must reproduce positive mixed
  1306	ranges, pinned witness substitution and exact where scope. Static snapshot
  1307	binding is an explicit syntax normalization, like where-guard lowering.
  1308	Word search stays a named unsupported model fragment; it must execute zero
  1309	Lean oracle cases, while native/device tests cover its existing words engine.
  1310	The direct-grant soundness theorem remains positive: negative-grant scalar
  1311	absence/aggregation, fallible-filter fallback and descriptor overflow have the
  1312	explicit `page-negative-grant-fallback` model boundary, including negative
  1313	probes nested in conjunctions and made static by prefix pins, and overflow of
  1314	distributed alternatives inside conjunctions, with zero oracle
  1315	executions. Standard bounded negative-grant pages use the independent range
  1316	classifier and are exercised against the model. No unsupported case is counted
  1317	as a pass. Fresh Muse and Codex reviews both mark semantic revision
  1318	`193f94ea9da7a046f4ce6fe76d7b527e1686ef90` READY. See the
  1319	[verbatim paired reviews and provenance](reviews/3000.036-page4.provenance.md).
  1320	Qualification is recorded separately in the PAGE4 workstream report.
  1321	
  1322	Model fidelity: lexical witness pins use an opaque classifier-only parameter
  1323	term, preserving variable identity instead of converting observed argument
  1324	values to literals or viewer proofs. The oracle rejects that term in authored
  1325	rule JSON; executable authority always evaluates the original rule. Named
  1326	plain orders normalize only within the independent unfiltered mixed-merge
  1327	fragment. Filtered/one-party named candidates outside that fragment (including declined
  1328	native candidates) have the explicit
  1329	`page-named-index-fallback` boundary and execute zero oracle cases. No such excluded candidate is claimed to be a native failure. This
  1330	retains native fallback semantics instead of claiming a complete owner pin
  1331	on an authored fixed index. Both review counterexamples have regressions;
  1332	the generator includes argument-valued pinned pairs.
  1333	
  1334	Delivered-history integration: an acquired device history is an audience in
  1335	its own right under LLP 3000.007 §18.2, even after live membership expires.
  1336	A membership merge on such a target selects its existing whole-range system
  1337	descriptor on the device without changing the actual viewer. It retains the
  1338	authored W, acquisition, lifetime, retention, meters and shared cursor scope;
  1339	only current-source rediscovery is bypassed. Server execution is unchanged.
  1340	`branch-pages-v3` fences this device interpretation. Existing expiry/paged
  1341	snapshot history regressions run on both stores. NARR retains ownership of
  1342	P9's general unbounded-sync/empty-partition narration; this compatibility
  1343	correction arose from PAGE4 named-order planning.
  1344	
  1345	## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies
  1346	
  1347	Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
  1348	planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
  1349	argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
  1350	query plan.
  1351	
  1352	**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
  1353	alternatives in the authorization rule across conjunctions before it
  1354	classifies the resulting lanes.
  1355	For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
  1356	alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
  1357	the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
  1358	inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
  1359	negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
  1360	analyzer-limitation fallback.
  1361	
  1362	The same bounded Cartesian construction applies to positive membership PAGE
  1363	query filters. Every resulting branch must contain an eligible equality pin,
  1364	the system descriptor plus every party or membership descriptor must remain
  1365	within eight, and every original predicate stays in its lane. A product that
  1366	pins the membership witness's varying outer field, overflows, or leaves a
  1367	branch without a usable pin stays on the ordinary incomplete scan. The Lean
  1368	classifier constructs every factor before applying those same exclusions; it
  1369	never treats a failed product as an undistributed complete lane or a second
  1370	`or` predicate as absent. A non-alternative equality or required bare Boolean
  1371	shared by every product branch supplies an eligible pin to each branch.
  1372	Repeated party leaves name one physical audience prefix and count once per
  1373	product branch.
  1374	
  1375	**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
  1376	be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored
  1377	conjunction grouping. A fixed guard is admissible only when
  1378	the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
  1379	required, total, unique point that is caller-readable after those exact lane
  1380	pins are substituted. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
  1381	`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
  1382	into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
  1383	an already-static point such as `exists roles[viewer]` needs no substitution
  1384	but remains in that filter. The residual may contain three or more fixed
  1385	guards; its exact short-circuit conjunction chain remains necessary in full.
  1386	The core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
  1387	and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
  1388	authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
  1389	guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
  1390	fragment.
  1391	
  1392	**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
  1393	`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
  1394	plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
  1395	same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
  1396	candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
  1397	authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
  1398	therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
  1399	outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and any shortcut
  1400	around the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the fallback. The target
  1401	may itself be hidden or timed: the private server walk evaluates the authored
  1402	rule's system probe for each candidate, then exposes only the authorized outer
  1403	row. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a private
  1404	target's absence is globally observable.
  1405	The intrinsic `id` point is always required and unique even though schema
  1406	column and unique lists omit it; other vacancy keys require both declarations.
  1407	
  1408	**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
  1409	independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
  1410	using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
  1411	rewritten query using that same index. The rewrite locates the focal read by
  1412	its source-map span, even when an earlier read uses the same index name, and
  1413	the binder recompiles the printed declaration and query together in tests.
  1414	It omits the repair when the added components or emitted label would exceed
  1415	the portable index bounds, or when authored bracket keys would change column
  1416	meaning under the prepended prefix.
  1417	When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
  1418	does not offer this rewrite.
  1419	
  1420	**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences the new validator
  1421	interpretations: fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device
  1422	that only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
  1423	executing it; bounded products reuse the already validated positive-branch
  1424	lanes, and existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean surface
  1425	classifier mirrors the three admissions and the private authorization walk.
  1426	Generated differential cases exercise the new lane families on native,
  1427	simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the fragment remain honest analyzer
  1428	limitations rather than completeness claims.

+/// Every sibling of a varying membership witness must already hold. Preserve

+fn fixed_rule_holds<'a>(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
+    outer_name: &str, outer_table: &Table, pinned: &impl Fn(&str) -> Option<&'a Expr>,
+    pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
+    if rule_true(rule, outer_name, outer_table, pinned) { return true; }
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::And(parts) => parts.iter().all(|part|
+            fixed_rule_holds(schema, filter, part, outer_name, outer_table, pinned, pins)),
+        RuleExpr::Or(parts) => parts.iter().any(|part|
+            fixed_rule_holds(schema, filter, part, outer_name, outer_table, pinned, pins)),
+        _ => fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, filter, rule, pins),
+    }
+}
+
+/// A generated membership lane may retain prefix-fixed point grants
 /// as a residual predicate.  Recognize only the exact bound point expression:
 /// this proves that every row emitted by the lane satisfies that conjunct,
 /// while a missing, reordered or merely similar probe keeps the merge out.
-// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — one fixed grant may guard one varying per-row grant.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — fixed grants may guard one varying per-row grant.
 fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
     pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
     if let Expr::Binary { op: crate::ir::BinaryOp::And, left, right } = filter {
         return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
             || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
     }
+    // The IR preserves short-circuit `a and b` as a tiny Boolean block.
+    // Accept only that exact lowering: both operands are necessary, while an
+    // `or` block assigns on the false branch and cannot establish either one.
+    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter
+        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
+            condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = body.as_slice()
+        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice()
+        && else_body.is_empty() && condition == name && assigned == name
+        && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(result) if result == name) {
+        return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
+            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+    }
     let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
     let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
     let Some(point) = target.index(index) else { return false };
     equalities.is_empty() && target.through.is_none() && target.not_before.is_none() && !target.is_expiring()
         && point.unique && point.is_plain() && point.components.iter().all(|part| part.column()
             .and_then(|column| target.columns.get(column)).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))
-        && schema.rule_input_is_readable(table, index, key)
         && &read.table == table && &read.index == index && read.key.len() == key.len()
         && read.key.iter().zip(key).all(|(expression, term)| rule_term_expression(term, expression, pins))
+        && point_read_is_visible(schema, read)
+}
+
+/// A rule probe uses system authority, but the retained residual point uses
+/// ordinary caller authority. Re-prove that the generated, pin-substituted
+/// point is visible instead of asking about the unsubstituted authored terms.
+fn point_read_is_visible(schema: &Schema, read: &crate::ir::Read) -> bool {
+    let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return false };
+    let Some(index) = table.index(&read.index) else { return false };
+    if !index.is_plain() || index.components.len() != read.key.len() { return false; }
+    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&read.key).find_map(|(part, value)|
+        (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then_some(value));
+    visibility(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned).decided_true
 }
 
 fn rule_term_expression(term: &RuleTerm, expression: &Expr, pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {

+
+/// A membership PAGE with two independent `or` filters stays on the ordinary
+/// incomplete scan. The native membership planner distributes at most one
+/// such filter; the independent model must not silently discard the second.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — undistributed query alternatives are outside the constructive PAGE fragment.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_fallback(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::OwnerOrWitness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };

+#[test]
+fn membership_page_with_two_filter_alternatives_stays_incomplete_in_lean_and_native() {
+    let have_model = model_built();
+    for seed in 0..4 {
+        let case = snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_filter_fallback(seed);
+        let source = case.program.source();
+        execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+        let mut engine = execute::engine(&case, &source).unwrap();
+        let outcome = execute::execute(&mut engine, &case, "run", &case.args);
+        assert_eq!(outcome.value["complete"], false, "seed {seed}: {outcome:?}\n{source}");
+        let mut report = Report::default(); campaign::check(&case, &mut report, |_| {});
+        assert!(!report.failed(), "seed {seed}: {report:?}\n{source}");
+        if have_model {
+            let checked = snapback4_difftest::lean::compare(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Pass), "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+        }
+    }
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs
index 8b4251ea02..4fe1a6b0f8 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs
@@ -992,10 +992,24 @@ fn equality_filter_index_rewrite(catalog: &Catalog, table: &snapback4_core::sche
     while table.indexes.contains_key(&name) {
         name = format!("by{stem}{suffix}"); suffix += 1;
     }
-    let declaration = declaration?;
-    let old = format!(" by {}", scan.index);
-    if !declaration.contains(&old) { return None; }
-    let rewritten = declaration.replacen(&old, &format!(" by {name}"), 1);
+    // Locate the authored read by its source-map span. A declaration can use
+    // the same old index more than once; replacing a text occurrence can edit
+    // an earlier, unrelated read and print a query that does not compile.
+    let read_site = read_site?;
+    let (mut read, text, grouped, start) = catalog.source.read(read_site)?;
+    if read.index.as_deref() != Some(scan.index.as_str()) { return None; }
+    read.index = Some(name.clone());
+    let fixed = crate::ast::Expr::Read(read);
+    let replacement = crate::print::with_source_at(&fixed, if grouped { 8 } else { 0 }, &|expr| catalog.source.get(expr));
+    let declaration = catalog.source.declarations.get(&declaration_site.to_string())?;
+    let line = start.line.checked_sub(declaration_site.line)? as usize;
+    let lines = declaration.split('\n').collect::<Vec<_>>();
+    let column = start.col.checked_sub(1)? as usize;
+    let byte = lines.get(line)?.char_indices().map(|(i, _)| i).chain([lines[line].len()]).nth(column)?;
+    let offset = lines[..line].iter().map(|line| line.len() + 1).sum::<usize>() + byte;
+    let span = offset..offset.checked_add(text.len())?;
+    if declaration.get(span.clone())? != text { return None; }
+    let rewritten = crate::parser::repairs::splice(declaration, vec![(span, replacement)])?;
     Some(format!("on table {} add `by {name}: {}`; use the same index in the rewritten query:\n```quarry\n{rewritten}\n```", scan.table, leading.join(", ")))
 }

@@ -1359,11 +1359,14 @@ inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
 negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
 analyzer-limitation fallback.
 
-This does not erase query predicates that the membership PAGE planner cannot
-distribute. It admits no query-filter alternative or one bounded positive
-alternative set. Two independent `or` filters stay on the ordinary incomplete
-scan even when each expression is total; the Lean classifier makes the same
-decline instead of treating those predicates as absent.
+The same bounded Cartesian construction applies to positive membership PAGE
+query filters. Every resulting branch must contain an eligible equality pin,
+the system descriptor plus every party or membership descriptor must remain
+within eight, and every original predicate stays in its lane. A product that
+pins the membership witness's varying outer field, overflows, or leaves a
+branch without a usable pin stays on the ordinary incomplete scan. The Lean
+classifier constructs every factor before applying those same exclusions; it
+never treats a second `or` predicate as absent.
 
 **Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
 be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored

-/// A membership PAGE with two independent `or` filters stays on the ordinary
-/// incomplete scan. The native membership planner distributes at most one
-/// such filter; the independent model must not silently discard the second.
-// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — undistributed query alternatives are outside the constructive PAGE fragment.
+/// A membership PAGE whose alternative product pins the membership's varying
+/// target stays on the ordinary incomplete scan. The independent model must
+/// classify every factor before applying the same pinned-target exclusion.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — a filter product cannot replace varying membership discovery.
 pub fn page_membership_filter_fallback(seed: u64) -> Case {
     let mut case = base(seed);
     case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::OwnerOrWitness;
@@ -543,3 +549,34 @@ pub fn page_membership_filter_fallback(seed: u64) -> Case {
     case.steps.clear(); case.rows.clear();
     case
 }
+
+/// Two independent equality-alternative factors are a bounded Cartesian
+/// membership plan when no alternative pins the membership's varying field.
+/// This mirrors the native planner in the independent Lean classifier.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — bounded positive query products retain every factor in each lane.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_product(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    let mut rng = Rng::new(seed ^ 0x5041_4745_355f_5052);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::Witness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("region", Type::Text));
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("status", Type::Text));
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1 + rng.pick(3) as u32,
+        descending: rng.pick(2) == 0, filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.domain = Some("(.region = 'north' or .region = 'south')".into());
+    case.program.residual = Some("(.status = 'open' or .status = 'held')".into());
+    case.program.order = Some(vec!["label".into(), "score".into()]);
+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear();
+    case.steps.clear();
+    for (table, row) in &mut case.rows {
+        if table == "items" {
+            row["region"] = json!(if rng.pick(3) == 0 { "other" } else if rng.pick(2) == 0 { "north" } else { "south" });
+            row["status"] = json!(if rng.pick(3) == 0 { "closed" } else if rng.pick(2) == 0 { "open" } else { "held" });
+        }
+    }
+    case
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
index 5b119543b2..7d6a20da18 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
@@ -777,3 +777,22 @@ fn membership_page_with_two_filter_alternatives_stays_incomplete_in_lean_and_nat
         }
     }
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn membership_page_with_two_equality_alternative_factors_is_complete_in_lean_and_native() {
+    let have_model = model_built();
+    for seed in 0..4 {
+        let case = snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_filter_product(seed);
+        let source = case.program.source();
+        execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+        let mut engine = execute::engine(&case, &source).unwrap();
+        let outcome = execute::execute(&mut engine, &case, "run", &case.args);
+        assert_eq!(outcome.value["complete"], true, "seed {seed}: {outcome:?}\n{source}");
+        let mut report = Report::default(); campaign::check(&case, &mut report, |_| {});
+        assert!(!report.failed(), "seed {seed}: {report:?}\n{source}");
+        if have_model {
+            let checked = snapback4_difftest::lean::compare(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Pass), "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+        }
+    }
+}

+/-- The native membership planner's bounded Cartesian product of positive
+query alternatives. Every resulting branch needs an eligible equality pin;
+the later case-specific descriptor check keeps the system lane plus all
+membership/party lanes within eight. -/
+def filterAlternatives (td : TableDecl) (fs : List Expr) : Option (List (List Expr)) :=
+  let factors := (fs.filter Expr.isOr).map fun e => e.disjuncts.map Expr.conjuncts
+  if factors.isEmpty then none else
+    let branches := factors.foldl (fun product factor =>
+      product.flatMap fun prior => factor.map fun alternative => prior ++ alternative) [[]]
+    if 2 ≤ branches.length && branches.length ≤ 8
+      && branches.all (fun branch => branch.any (eligiblePin td))
+    then some branches else none
+
 /-- The branch rule of LLP 3000.036.001 §2 (cases 1 and 2; a grant branch is
 complete for its holders as `granted` is, and empty for everyone else). -/

@@ -708,13 +723,7 @@ def branchDecided (sch : Schema) (t : String) (pins : List (String × Expr))

 pins the membership witness's varying outer field, overflows, or leaves a

 classifier constructs every factor before applying those same exclusions; it
-never treats a second `or` predicate as absent.
+never treats a failed product as an undistributed complete lane or a second
+`or` predicate as absent. A non-alternative equality shared by every product
+branch supplies an eligible pin to each branch.
 
 **Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
 be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored
@@ -1376,7 +1378,10 @@ required, total, unique point that is caller-readable after those exact lane
 pins are substituted. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,

 into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;

@@ -1395,6 +1400,8 @@ may itself be hidden or timed: the private server walk evaluates the authored

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs
index 1da7c11948..240e06a945 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs
@@ -805,17 +805,31 @@ fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
         return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
             || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
     }
-    // The IR preserves short-circuit `a and b` as a tiny Boolean block.
-    // Accept only that exact lowering: both operands are necessary, while an
-    // `or` block assigns on the false branch and cannot establish either one.
-    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter
-        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
-            condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = body.as_slice()
-        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice()
-        && else_body.is_empty() && condition == name && assigned == name
-        && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(result) if result == name) {
-        return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
-            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+    // The compiler lowers a left-associated `a and b and c` into successive
+    // Let/If pairs. Accept only that exact dataflow chain: every right operand
+    // assigns the current result on its true branch, while `or` assigns on the
+    // false branch and any extra statement or mismatched name is rejected.
+    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter {
+        if body.is_empty() || body.len() % 2 != 0 { return false; }
+        let mut previous: Option<&String> = None;
+        let mut matched = false;
+        let mut at = 0;
+        while at < body.len() {
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
+                condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = &body[at..at + 2] else { return false };
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice() else { return false };
+            if !else_body.is_empty() || condition != name || assigned != name { return false; }
+            if let Some(previous) = previous {
+                if !matches!(left, Expr::Variable(input) if input == previous) { return false; }
+            } else {
+                matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins);
+            }
+            matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+            previous = Some(name);
+            at += 2;
+        }
+        if !matches!((value.as_ref(), previous), (Expr::Variable(result), Some(previous)) if result == previous) { return false; }
+        return matched;
     }
     let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
     let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };

+
+/// Four binary equality factors require sixteen membership lanes and must not
+/// collapse into one undistributed complete descriptor after product overflow.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — alternative overflow retains the ordinary incomplete scan.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_overflow(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::Witness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    for name in ["p", "q", "r", "s"] { case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column(name, Type::Int)); }
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.domain = Some("(.p = 0 or .p = 1) and (.q = 0 or .q = 1) and (.r = 0 or .r = 1) and (.s = 0 or .s = 1)".into());
+    case.program.residual = None; case.program.order = Some(vec!["label".into(), "score".into()]);
+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear(); case.steps.clear();
+    for (table, row) in &mut case.rows { if table == "items" {
+        for (at, name) in ["p", "q", "r", "s"].into_iter().enumerate() { row[name] = json!(((seed as usize + at) % 3) as i64); }
+    }}
+    case
+}
+
+/// A shared equality supplies the physical prefix for every Cartesian lane;
+/// the alternative factors themselves may remain total residual comparisons.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — common positive pins count in every product branch.
+pub fn page_membership_shared_filter_product(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::Witness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("region", Type::Text));
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("rank", Type::Int));
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.domain = Some(".region = 'north'".into());
+    case.program.residual = Some("(.score > 0 or .score > 10) and (.rank > 0 or .rank > 10)".into());
+    case.program.order = Some(vec!["label".into(), "score".into()]);
+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear(); case.steps.clear();
+    for (table, row) in &mut case.rows { if table == "items" {
+        row["region"] = json!(if seed % 3 == 0 { "south" } else { "north" });
+        row["rank"] = json!((seed % 15) as i64);
+    }}
+    case
+}
+
+/// Vacancy may use the target table's always-present required unique id even
+/// though the explicit column/unique lists omit that intrinsic point.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — implicit id is a required exact vacancy point.
+pub fn page5_implicit_id_vacancy(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = page5(seed, 2);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::VacancyImplicitId;
+    case
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
index 7d6a20da18..f27427ef2c 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
@@ -796,3 +796,33 @@ fn membership_page_with_two_equality_alternative_factors_is_complete_in_lean_and
         }
     }
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn page5_review_boundaries_agree_in_generated_native_and_lean_cases() {
+    let have_model = model_built();
+    for seed in 0..4 {
+        for (case, complete, model_boundary) in [
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_filter_overflow(seed), false, Some("page-membership-filter-product")),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_shared_filter_product(seed), true, None),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page5_implicit_id_vacancy(seed), true, None),
+        ] {
+            let source = case.program.source();
+            execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+            let mut engine = execute::engine(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            let outcome = execute::execute(&mut engine, &case, "run", &case.args);
+            assert_eq!(outcome.value["complete"], complete, "seed {seed}: {outcome:?}\n{source}");
+            let mut report = Report::default(); campaign::check(&case, &mut report, |_| {});
+            assert!(!report.failed(), "seed {seed}: {report:?}\n{source}");
+            if have_model {
+                let checked = snapback4_difftest::lean::compare(&case, &source).unwrap();
+                if let Some(reason) = model_boundary {
+                    assert!(matches!(&checked.verdict, Verdict::Unsupported(actual) if actual == reason),
+                        "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                    assert_eq!(checked.executed, 0, "seed {seed}: model boundary executed\n{source}");
+                } else {
+                    assert!(matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Pass), "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs
index d78d99500b..47e31e0e1f 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs
@@ -1,6 +1,54 @@
 //! Physical page eligibility derived solely from Quarry surface declarations.
 use super::*;
 
+enum PagePins {
+    Plain(Vec<String>),
+    Product { system: Vec<String>, branches: Vec<Vec<String>> },
+    Invalid,
+}
+
+/// Reconstruct the native planner's bounded Cartesian product from the
+/// authored filter.  This stays surface-derived: the executable model cannot
+/// trust the binder's proof metadata when deciding whether comparison is safe.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — every modeled PAGE filter lane has its own exact physical prefix.
+fn page_pins(table: &Table, read: &ReadExpr) -> PagePins {
+    let Some(filter) = read.filter.as_deref() else { return PagePins::Plain(Vec::new()) };
+    let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
+    flatten_and(filter, &mut conjuncts);
+    let system = model_pin_columns(table, &conjuncts, &read.order_keys);
+    let mut branches = vec![Vec::new()];
+    let mut distributed = false;
+    for conjunct in &conjuncts {
+        let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
+        flatten_or(conjunct, &mut alternatives);
+        distributed |= alternatives.len() > 1;
+        if branches.len().saturating_mul(alternatives.len()) > 8 { return PagePins::Invalid; }
+        branches = branches.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix| alternatives.iter().map(move |alternative| {
+            let mut lane = prefix.clone();
+            flatten_and(alternative, &mut lane);
+            lane
+        })).collect();
+    }
+    if !distributed || branches.len() < 2 { return PagePins::Plain(system); }
+    if branches.iter().any(|branch| !branch.iter().any(|atom| page_pin(table, atom))) {
+        return PagePins::Invalid;
+    }
+    PagePins::Product {
+        system,
+        branches: branches.into_iter().map(|branch| model_pin_columns(table, &branch, &read.order_keys)).collect(),
+    }
+}
+
+fn page_pin(table: &Table, expression: &Expr) -> bool {
+    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = expression else { return false };
+    if op != "=" { return false; }
+    let (column, value) = if let Some(column) = column_of(left) { (column, right.as_ref()) }

+#[test]
+fn lean_gate_prices_every_filter_product_pin_before_comparison() {
+    let source = "table access:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  principal: principal\n  unique bucket, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable items:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  region: text <=435\n  status: text <=435\n  at: int\n  read <- exists access[.bucket, viewer]\n  online only\nquery mine(c: cursor ?):\n  return items where (.region = 'north' or .region = 'south') and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'held') first 10 by .at after c\n";
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    assert!(snapback4_core::ir::body_merges(&bound.programs[0].program.body)
+        .iter().all(|merge| !merge.branches()), "{:?}", bound.programs);
+    let module = snapback4_lang::emit::lean::lean(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(module.program("mine").unwrap_err().reason, "page-membership-key-bound");
+}

 ## 0. Summary
@@ -1341,3 +1341,60 @@ only current-source rediscovery is bypassed. Server execution is unchanged.
 snapshot history regressions run on both stores. NARR retains ownership of
 P9's general unbounded-sync/empty-partition narration; this compatibility
 correction arose from PAGE4 named-order planning.
+
+## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies
+
+Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
+planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
+argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
+query plan.
+
+**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
+alternatives across conjunctions before it classifies the resulting lanes.
+For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
+alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
+the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
+inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
+negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
+analyzer-limitation fallback.
+
+**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
+be accompanied by fixed `exists` guards. A fixed guard is admissible only when
+the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
+required, total, unique point that is readable under the target's system
+rule. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
+`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
+into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
+the core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
+and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
+authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
+guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
+fragment.
+
+**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
+`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
+plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
+same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
+candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
+authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
+therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
+outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and hidden/timed
+interpretations outside the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the
+fallback. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a
+private target's absence is globally observable.
+
+**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
+independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
+using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
+rewritten query using that same index. The binder recompiles that repair in
+tests. When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
+does not offer this rewrite.
+
+**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences bounded conjunctive
+products, fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device that
+only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
+executing it; existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean
+surface classifier mirrors the exact vacancy admission predicate and the
+private authorization walk. Generated differential cases exercise the new
+lane families on native, simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the
+fragment remain honest analyzer limitations rather than completeness claims.
