    65	/// Bring this checkout's `quarry-oracle` up to date with its model sources
    66	/// (`lake build quarry-oracle`, under a second when current). A merge or
    67	/// checkout that changes `lean/` leaves the previous executable in `.lake`,
    68	/// and the harness would report that older model's choices as disagreements
    69	/// (2026-10-12: a stale pre-guard oracle on the merged `sb4-loop` tree showed
    70	/// `lean--complete--complete~incomplete`). Nothing is done when
    71	/// `SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE` names the oracle (the caller vouches for it; a
    72	/// campaign's workers inherit the refreshed path that way) or when `lake` is
    73	/// not installed (a missing oracle still refuses at `LeanProcess::spawn`).
    74	pub fn refresh() -> Result<(), String> {
    75	    if std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE").is_some() {
    76	        return Ok(());
    77	    }
    78	    let dir = PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("../../lean");
    79	    // Keep the optional-tool path read-only, including a checkout whose model
    80	    // directory is not writable. A version probe writes no Lake build artifacts.
    81	    match Command::new("lake").arg("--version").current_dir(&dir).stdin(Stdio::null()).output() {
    82	        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
    83	            eprintln!("lake not found: using the Lean oracle as built at {}", oracle_path().display());
    84	            return Ok(());
    85	        }
    86	        Err(e) => return Err(format!("lake: {e}")),
    87	        Ok(_) => {} // The build below reports actual toolchain/configuration errors.
    88	    }
    89	    // @ref LLP 3000.035#52-stage-2-a-small-lean-core — concurrent refreshes must not write the same Lake artifacts.
    90	    let cache = dir.join(".lake");
    91	    fs::create_dir_all(&cache).map_err(|e| format!("Lean refresh cache: {e}"))?;
    92	    let refresh_lock = OpenOptions::new().create(true).truncate(false).read(true).write(true)
    93	        .open(cache.join("snapback4-refresh.lock")).map_err(|e| format!("Lean refresh lock: {e}"))?;
    94	    refresh_lock.lock().map_err(|e| format!("Lean refresh lock: {e}"))?;
    95	    match Command::new("lake").args(["build", "quarry-oracle"]).current_dir(&dir).stdin(Stdio::null()).output() {
    96	        Ok(output) if output.status.success() => Ok(()),
    97	        Ok(output) => Err(format!(
    98	            "`lake build quarry-oracle` failed in {}: {}{}",
    99	            dir.display(),
   100	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout),
   101	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
   102	        )),
   103	        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
   104	            eprintln!("lake not found: using the Lean oracle as built at {}", oracle_path().display());
   105	            Ok(())
   106	        }
   107	        Err(e) => Err(format!("lake: {e}")),
   108	    }
   109	}

   111	/// One `quarry-oracle` process: a JSON case per line in, an outcome per line out.
   112	pub struct LeanProcess {
   113	    child: Child,
   114	    stdin: ChildStdin,
   115	    stdout: BufReader<ChildStdout>,
   116	}
   117	impl LeanProcess {
   118	    pub fn spawn() -> Result<Self, String> {
   119	        let path = oracle_path();
   120	        if !path.is_file() {
   121	            return Err(format!(
   122	                "Lean oracle missing: {} (run `cd snapback4/lean && lake build`, or set SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE)",
   123	                path.display()
   124	            ));
   125	        }
   126	        let mut child = Command::new(&path)
   127	            .stdin(Stdio::piped())
   128	            .stdout(Stdio::piped())
   129	            .stderr(Stdio::inherit())
   130	            .spawn()
   131	            .map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle {}: {e}", path.display()))?;
   132	        let stdin = child.stdin.take().unwrap();
   133	        let stdout = BufReader::new(child.stdout.take().unwrap());
   134	        Ok(Self { child, stdin, stdout })
   135	    }
   136	    pub fn ask(&mut self, case: &Json) -> Result<Json, String> {
   137	        writeln!(self.stdin, "{case}").map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle write: {e}"))?;
   138	        self.stdin.flush().map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle flush: {e}"))?;
   139	        let mut line = String::new();
   140	        let n = self.stdout.read_line(&mut line).map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle read: {e}"))?;
   141	        if n == 0 {
   142	            return Err("Lean oracle exited".into());
   143	        }
   144	        let answer: Json = serde_json::from_str(&line).map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle answer {line:?}: {e}"))?;
   145	        if answer["kind"] == "error" {
   146	            // A case the model cannot decode is an emitter defect, never a verdict.
   147	            return Err(format!("Lean oracle refused the case: {}", answer["error"]));
   148	        }
   149	        Ok(answer)
   150	    }
   151	}
   152	impl Drop for LeanProcess {
   153	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   154	        let _ = self.child.kill();
   155	        let _ = self.child.wait();
   156	    }
   157	}
   158	
   159	thread_local! {
   160	    static PROCESS: RefCell<Option<LeanProcess>> = const { RefCell::new(None) };
   161	}
   162	
   163	/// Ask this thread's oracle process, starting it on first use and once
   164	/// more if it has died.
   165	pub fn ask(case: &Json) -> Result<Json, String> {
   166	    PROCESS.with(|cell| {
   167	        let mut slot = cell.borrow_mut();
   168	        if slot.is_none() {
   169	            *slot = Some(LeanProcess::spawn()?);
   170	        }
   171	        match slot.as_mut().unwrap().ask(case) {
   172	            Err(e) if e == "Lean oracle exited" => {
   173	                *slot = Some(LeanProcess::spawn()?);
   174	                slot.as_mut().unwrap().ask(case)
   175	            }

     6	pub(super) struct AudienceNeed {
     7	    pub prefix: Vec<String>,
     8	    pub inverse: Option<(String, Vec<String>)>,

    10	
    11	// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — planning consumes the same admitted rule/index order as sync binding.
    12	/// One need per arm: a single audience has one; a union one per disjunct
    13	/// (LLP 3000.036.000.000 §2.1), each with its own prefix index. Union windows are refused.
    14	pub(super) fn needs(table: &TableInfo, catalog: &Catalog) -> Vec<AudienceNeed> {
    15	    let empty = || AudienceNeed { prefix: Vec::new(), inverse: None };
    16	    let Some(sync) = &table.decl.sync else { return vec![empty()]; };
    17	    let rule = table.decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == Effect::Read)
    18	        .and_then(|rule| super::schema::bind_rule(catalog, table, rule).ok())
    19	        .map(|rule| audience_rule(&table.decl.name, table, &rule));
    20	    let probe = |rule: &RuleExpr| -> Option<AudienceNeed> {
    21	        let RuleExpr::Exists { table: target, index, key, equalities, .. } = rule else { return None };
    22	        if !equalities.is_empty() || key.last() != Some(&RuleTerm::Viewer) { return None; }
    23	        let index = catalog.tables.get(target).and_then(|t| t.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == index))?;
    24	        if !index.is_plain() || index.columns.len() != key.len() { return None; }
    25	        let prefix = key[..key.len()-1].iter().map(|term| match term {
    26	            RuleTerm::Field(column) => Some(column.clone()), _ => None,
    27	        }).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()?;
    28	        let mut inverse = vec![index.columns.last().unwrap().clone()];
    29	        inverse.extend(index.columns[..index.columns.len()-1].iter().cloned());
    30	        Some(AudienceNeed { prefix, inverse: Some((target.clone(), inverse)) })
    31	    };
    32	    let column = |rule: &RuleExpr| match rule {
    33	        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) => Some(column.clone()),
    34	        _ => None,
    35	    };
    36	    if let Some(rule) = &rule {
    37	        let disjuncts = rule.disjuncts();
    38	        // A union whose every disjunct is an arm plans every arm.
    39	        if disjuncts.len() > 1 && !matches!(sync.audience, SyncAudience::Public) {
    40	            let arms: Option<Vec<AudienceNeed>> = disjuncts.iter().map(|disjunct| probe(disjunct)
    41	                .or_else(|| column(disjunct).map(|column| AudienceNeed { prefix: vec![column], inverse: None }))).collect();
    42	            if let Some(mut arms) = arms {
    43	                // The group arm first, as the bound declaration orders it.
    44	                arms.sort_by_key(|arm| arm.inverse.is_none());
    45	                arms.dedup_by(|a, b| a.prefix == b.prefix && a.inverse == b.inverse);
    46	                return arms;
    47	            }
    48	        }
    49	        if let Some(need) = probe(rule) { return vec![need]; }
    50	    }

    52	        SyncAudience::Column(column) => vec![column.clone()],
    53	        SyncAudience::Target { prefix, .. } => prefix.clone(),
    54	        SyncAudience::Inferred { .. } => rule.as_ref().and_then(column).map(|column| vec![column]).unwrap_or_default(),
    55	        SyncAudience::Public | SyncAudience::Union(_) => Vec::new(),
    56	    };
    57	    vec![AudienceNeed { prefix, inverse: None }]

    60	/// The read rule as a sync audience: service grants removed, then each
    61	/// same-row `.principal = viewer` disjunct that a self-probe on a total
    62	/// unique key absorbs (the row is its own witness). The core's
    63	/// `sync_audience_rule` makes the same simplification and remains the authority.
    64	// @ref LLP 3000.036.000#3.1 — exact equivalence only; anything else stays a refused union.
    65	pub(super) fn audience_rule(name: &str, info: &TableInfo, rule: &RuleExpr) -> RuleExpr {
    66	    let audience = rule.device_audience_rule().into_owned();
    67	    let terms = audience.disjuncts();
    68	    if terms.len() < 2 { return audience; }
    69	    let absorbs = |disjunct: &RuleExpr, other: &RuleExpr| {
    70	        let (RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column))) = disjunct else { return false };
    71	        let RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } = other else { return false };
    72	        let Some(index) = info.indexes.iter().find(|candidate| &candidate.name == index) else { return false };
    73	        table == name && info.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() == key.len() && index.columns.contains(column)
    74	            && equalities.iter().all(|(field, term)| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(source) if source == field) || (field == column && matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer)))
    75	            && index.columns.iter().zip(key).all(|(component, term)|
    76	                if component == column { matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer) } else { matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(field) if field == component) })
    77	    };
    78	    let mut kept: Vec<RuleExpr> = Vec::new();
    79	    for term in terms.iter().filter(|term| !terms.iter().any(|other| absorbs(term, other))) {
    80	        // A repeated branch is one audience, as the core folds it.
    81	        if !kept.iter().any(|seen| seen.normal_disjunct() == term.normal_disjunct()) { kept.push((*term).clone()); }
    82	    }
    83	    match kept.len() {
    84	        n if n == terms.len() => audience.clone(),
    85	        1 => kept.into_iter().next().expect("one audience"),
    86	        _ => RuleExpr::Or(kept),
    87	    }
    88	}

     1	//! A declaration must fit the same encoded index key on every store.

    10	
    11	fn column_bytes(kind: &ColumnType) -> usize {
    12	    match kind {
    13	        ColumnType::Optional(inner) => column_bytes(inner),
    14	        ColumnType::Id | ColumnType::Principal | ColumnType::Ref { .. } => ID_BYTES,
    15	        ColumnType::Text { max } => (*max as usize).saturating_mul(4).saturating_add(3),
    16	        ColumnType::Bytes { max } => (*max as usize).saturating_mul(2).saturating_add(3),
    17	        ColumnType::Enum { variants } => variants.iter().map(|v| key::encode(&[Value::String(v.clone())]).len()).max().unwrap_or(1),

    19	        ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::IntRange { .. } | ColumnType::Decimal { .. } | ColumnType::Money { .. } | ColumnType::Time => 9,

    21	        _ => usize::MAX,
    22	    }

    28	pub(super) fn plain_index_fits(table: &super::catalog::TableInfo, index: &crate::ast::IndexDecl, emitted_name: &str) -> bool {
    29	    table.decl.name.len().saturating_add(emitted_name.len()) <= MAX_INDEX_LABEL_BYTES

     3	
     4	/// A union of audiences (LLP 3000.036.000.000 §2.2): the audience rule is

     7	// @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#2.2 — every disjunct is one arm and every arm one disjunct, so each partition's recipients are readers and every reader has a partition.
     8	pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, table: &Table, sync: &Sync, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
     9	    let issue = |issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>, message: String| issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message });
    10	    let arms: Vec<(&Audience, Option<&Horizon>)> = sync.arms().collect();

   337	/// Compare one case whose source compiles. `Err` is a harness or emitter
   338	/// defect (never a verdict).
   339	pub fn compare(case: &Case, source: &str) -> Result<Checked, String> {
   340	    let unsupported = |reason: String| Ok(Checked { verdict: Verdict::Unsupported(reason), executed: 0, pages: 0, mutation: false, class: String::new() });
   341	    let module = match module(source) {
   342	        Ok(m) => m,
   343	        Err(reason) => return unsupported(reason),
   344	    };
   345	    let mutation = case.program.mutation();
   346	    if let Err(u) = module.program("run") {
   347	        return unsupported(u.reason);
   348	    }
   349	    if mutation {
   350	        if let Err(u) = module.program("observe") {
   351	            return unsupported(format!("observe-{}", u.reason));
   352	        }
   353	    }
   354	    let store = match module.store(&case.rows) {
   355	        Ok(s) => s,
   356	        Err(u) => return unsupported(u.reason),
   357	    };
   358	    let mut m_args = match module.tag_args("run", &case.args) {
   359	        Ok(a) => a,
   360	        Err(u) => return unsupported(u.reason),
   361	    };
   362	    // The prelude: each step on the engine and on the model's store, in order.
   363	    for i in 0..case.steps.len() {
   364	        if let Err(u) = module.program(&format!("prep{i}")) {
   365	            return unsupported(format!("prelude-{}", u.reason));
   366	        }
   367	        if let Err(u) = module.tag_args(&format!("prep{i}"), &case.steps[i].args) {
   368	            return unsupported(format!("prelude-{}", u.reason));
   369	        }
   370	    }
   371	    let (mut engine, prelude) = execute::world(case, source)?;
   372	    engine.set_ceilings(Ceilings::default());
   373	    let mut executed = 0;
   374	    let mut pages = 0;
   375	    let mut store = store;
   376	    let mut steps = prelude.iter();
   377	    let mut trail = vec![];
   378	    for (i, step) in case.steps.iter().enumerate() {
   379	        let name = format!("prep{i}");
   380	        if step.sweep {
   381	            let e_sweep = steps.next().ok_or("prelude sweep outcome missing")?;
   382	            if e_sweep.kind != "sweep" || e_sweep.value.get("error").is_some() {
   383	                return Err(format!("engine prelude sweep failed: {:?}", e_sweep.value));
   384	            }
   385	            let args = module.tag_args(&name, &step.args).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?;
   386	            let viewer = step.viewer.as_deref().unwrap_or(&case.principal);
   387	            let swept = ask(&module.case_at_sweep(&name, store.clone(), viewer, args, None, step.at, true).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?)?;
   388	            executed += 1;
   389	            if swept["kind"] != "committed" { return Err(format!("Lean prelude sweep failed: {swept}")); }
   390	            store = swept["store"].clone();
   391	        }
   392	        let e_raw = steps.next().ok_or("prelude outcome missing")?.clone();
   393	        let args = module.tag_args(&name, &step.args).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?;
   394	        let viewer = step.viewer.as_deref().unwrap_or(&case.principal);
   395	        let m_raw = ask(&module.case_at(&name, store.clone(), viewer, args, None, step.at).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?)?;
   396	        executed += 2;
   397	        let (e, m) = (engine_canon(&e_raw), model_canon(&m_raw));
   398	        trail.push(trail_entry(&e, &m));
   399	        if let Some(f) = differ(&e, &m) {
   400	            return Ok(Checked { verdict: finding(&format!("prelude-{f}"), &e, &m, e_raw, &m_raw, trail), executed, pages, mutation, class: String::new() });
   401	        }
   402	        if m.kind == "committed" {
   403	            store = m_raw["store"].clone();
   404	        }
   405	    }
   406	    if case.sweep {
   407	        let e_sweep = steps.next().ok_or("final sweep outcome missing")?;
   408	        if e_sweep.kind != "sweep" || e_sweep.value.get("error").is_some() {
   409	            return Err(format!("engine final sweep failed: {:?}", e_sweep.value));
   410	        }
   411	        let swept = ask(&module.case_at_sweep("run", store.clone(), &case.principal, m_args.clone(), None, case.clock, true).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?)?;
   412	        executed += 1;
   413	        if swept["kind"] != "committed" { return Err(format!("Lean final sweep failed: {swept}")); }
   414	        store = swept["store"].clone();
   415	    }
   416	    if mutation {
   417	        let e_raw = execute::execute(&mut engine, case, "run", &case.args);
   418	        let m_raw = ask(&module.case_at("run", store.clone(), &case.principal, m_args, None, case.clock).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?)?;
   419	        executed += 2;
   420	        let (e, m) = (engine_canon(&e_raw), model_canon(&m_raw));
   421	        let first_class = e.class("kind");
   422	        let mut trail = vec![trail_entry(&e, &m)];
   423	        if let Some(f) = differ(&e, &m) {
   424	            return Ok(Checked { verdict: finding(f, &e, &m, e_raw, &m_raw, trail), executed, pages, mutation, class: String::new() });
   425	        }
   426	        // The resulting stores, seen through `observe`.
   427	        let after = if m.kind == "committed" { m_raw["store"].clone() } else { store };
   428	        let o_args = module.tag_args("observe", &case.args).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?;
   429	        let eo_raw = execute::execute(&mut engine, case, "observe", &case.args);
   430	        let mo_raw = ask(&module.case_at("observe", after, &case.principal, o_args, None, case.clock).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?)?;
   431	        executed += 2;
   432	        let (eo, mo) = (engine_canon(&eo_raw), model_canon(&mo_raw));
   433	        trail.push(trail_entry(&eo, &mo));
   434	        if let Some(f) = differ(&eo, &mo) {
   435	            return Ok(Checked { verdict: finding(&format!("post-{f}"), &eo, &mo, eo_raw, &mo_raw, trail), executed, pages, mutation, class: String::new() });
   436	        }
   437	        return Ok(Checked { verdict: Verdict::Pass, executed, pages, mutation, class: format!("{}-{}", if mutation { "mutation" } else { "query" }, first_class) });
   438	    }
   439	    let mut e_args = case.args.clone();
   440	    let mut trail = Vec::new();
   441	    let mut seen = BTreeSet::new();
   442	    let mut first_class = String::new();
   443	    loop {
   444	        let e_raw = execute::execute(&mut engine, case, "run", &e_args);
   445	        let m_raw = ask(&module.case_at("run", store.clone(), &case.principal, m_args.clone(), None, case.clock).map_err(|u| u.to_string())?)?;
   446	        executed += 2;
   447	        pages += 1;
   448	        let (e, m) = (engine_canon(&e_raw), model_canon(&m_raw));
   449	        if pages == 1 {
   450	            first_class = e.class("kind");
   451	        }
   452	        trail.push(trail_entry(&e, &m));
   453	        let phase = if pages > 1 { "continuation-" } else { "" };
   454	        if let Some(f) = differ(&e, &m) {
   455	            return Ok(Checked { verdict: finding(&format!("{phase}{f}"), &e, &m, e_raw, &m_raw, trail), executed, pages, mutation, class: String::new() });
   456	        }
   457	        if !e.next {
   458	            break;
   459	        }
   460	        let next = e_raw.next().unwrap().to_owned();

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs
index d78d99500b..47e31e0e1f 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs
@@ -1,6 +1,54 @@
 //! Physical page eligibility derived solely from Quarry surface declarations.
 use super::*;
 
+enum PagePins {
+    Plain(Vec<String>),
+    Product { system: Vec<String>, branches: Vec<Vec<String>> },
+    Invalid,
+}
+
+/// Reconstruct the native planner's bounded Cartesian product from the
+/// authored filter.  This stays surface-derived: the executable model cannot
+/// trust the binder's proof metadata when deciding whether comparison is safe.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — every modeled PAGE filter lane has its own exact physical prefix.
+fn page_pins(table: &Table, read: &ReadExpr) -> PagePins {
+    let Some(filter) = read.filter.as_deref() else { return PagePins::Plain(Vec::new()) };
+    let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
+    flatten_and(filter, &mut conjuncts);
+    let system = model_pin_columns(table, &conjuncts, &read.order_keys);
+    let mut branches = vec![Vec::new()];
+    let mut distributed = false;
+    for conjunct in &conjuncts {
+        let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
+        flatten_or(conjunct, &mut alternatives);
+        distributed |= alternatives.len() > 1;
+        if branches.len().saturating_mul(alternatives.len()) > 8 { return PagePins::Invalid; }
+        branches = branches.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix| alternatives.iter().map(move |alternative| {
+            let mut lane = prefix.clone();
+            flatten_and(alternative, &mut lane);
+            lane
+        })).collect();
+    }
+    if !distributed || branches.len() < 2 { return PagePins::Plain(system); }
+    if branches.iter().any(|branch| !branch.iter().any(|atom| page_pin(table, atom))) {
+        return PagePins::Invalid;
+    }
+    PagePins::Product {
+        system,
+        branches: branches.into_iter().map(|branch| model_pin_columns(table, &branch, &read.order_keys)).collect(),
+    }
+}
+
+fn page_pin(table: &Table, expression: &Expr) -> bool {
+    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = expression else { return false };
+    if op != "=" { return false; }
+    let (column, value) = if let Some(column) = column_of(left) { (column, right.as_ref()) }
+        else if let Some(column) = column_of(right) { (column, left.as_ref()) }
+        else { return false };
+    column != "id" && model_independent(value) && table.cols.iter().find(|field| field.name == column)
+        .is_some_and(|field| !field.nullable || matches!(value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer"))
+}
+
 /// The executable predicate classifier currently knows literal/viewer point

    43	                A::Name { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => true,
    44	                A::Unary { value, .. } => stable(value),
    45	                A::Binary { left, right, .. } => stable(left) && stable(right),
    46	                A::Call { name, args, .. } if matches!(name.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket" | "month" | "year" | "monthStart" | "yearStart") => args.iter().all(stable),
    47	                _ => false,
    48	            }
    49	        }
    50	        stable(value).then_some((Some(value), 0))
    51	    }
    52	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#14 — ruling 28's dynamic widths stay meter-bounded; only structurally equal stable bases admit offset folding.
    53	    // Source locations do not matter, but precedence does: the printer is not
    54	    // an identity for expressions such as (a + b) * 2 and a + b * 2.
    55	    fn same(a: &A, b: &A) -> bool {
    56	        match (a, b) {
    57	            (A::Name { name: a, .. }, A::Name { name: b, .. }) => a == b,
    58	            (A::Literal { value: a, .. }, A::Literal { value: b, .. }) => a == b,
    59	            (A::Unary { op: a, value: av, .. }, A::Unary { op: b, value: bv, .. }) => a == b && same(av, bv),
    60	            (A::Binary { op: a, left: al, right: ar, .. }, A::Binary { op: b, left: bl, right: br, .. }) => a == b && same(al, bl) && same(ar, br),
    61	            (A::Call { name: a, args: av, .. }, A::Call { name: b, args: bv, .. }) => a == b && av.len() == bv.len() && av.iter().zip(bv).all(|(a, b)| same(a, b)),
    62	            _ => false,
    63	        }
    64	    }
    65	    let A::Call { args, .. } = domain else { return None };
    66	    let (a, start) = affine(&args[0])?;
    67	    let (b, end) = affine(&args[1])?;
    68	    match (a, b) {
    69	        (None, None) => Some(width(start, end)),
    70	        (Some(a), Some(b)) if same(a, b) => Some(width(start, end)),
    71	        _ => None,
    72	    }
    73	}
    74	
    75	// A lane-dependent predicate needs the eager binding snapshot. Plain row
    76	// predicates can keep the same head strategy as explicitly written lanes.
    77	pub(super) fn filter_uses_lane(value: &A, name: &str) -> bool {
    78	    let read_uses_lane = |read: &ast::ReadExpr| {
    79	        read.keys.iter().any(|key| filter_uses_lane(&key.value, name))
    80	            || read.seeks.iter().any(|seek| filter_uses_lane(&seek.value, name))
    81	            || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|filter| filter_uses_lane(filter, name))
    82	            || read.matching.as_deref().is_some_and(|matching| filter_uses_lane(matching, name))
    83	            || read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref())
    84	                .is_some_and(|cursor| filter_uses_lane(cursor, name))
    85	            || read.computed.values().any(|value| filter_uses_lane(value, name))
    86	    };
    87	    match value {
    88	        A::Name { name: used, .. } => used == name,
    89	        A::Focal { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => false,
    90	        A::Member { base, .. } | A::Unary { value: base, .. } => filter_uses_lane(base, name),
    91	        A::Binary { left, right, .. } => filter_uses_lane(left, name) || filter_uses_lane(right, name),
    92	        A::Call { args, .. } => args.iter().any(|arg| filter_uses_lane(arg, name)),
    93	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — an independent fixed point guard does
    94	        // not need a snapshot of the varying membership row.
    95	        A::Exists { read, .. } | A::Created { read, .. } | A::Read(read) => read_uses_lane(read),
    96	        _ => true,
    97	    }
    98	}

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
